People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um… it’s mostly because I keep hearing this man’s voice, and it’s gotten to where I can’t just brush it off anymore. It happens at work a lot, in the garage. It sounds like it’s coming through the vents, like from the ductwork, but then other times I’ll be leaning over a car and it sounds like it’s coming from under the hood, clear as if a guy is standing right next to me talking.

And it’s not just noise or like I’m imagining someone called my name. It’s actual words. He tells me not to trust my husband, and sometimes he tells me not to touch certain cars, like there’s something wrong or dangerous about them. I know how that sounds, I really do, but in the moment it feels so real that I freeze up and start looking around the shop trying to figure out where he is. It’s been scaring me, and it’s making it hard to do my job.

It’s been… about ten months, I think. Not years and years, but not just recently either. Yeah… that’s the part that scares me, because it doesn’t only happen when I’m all revved up or mad. There were a few weeks where I was quieter, like I wasn’t making all these huge plans or acting like I could take on everything. I was sleeping more, too, so I thought maybe things were settling down.

But even then, I still heard him. The garage voice. It would still come through like from the vents or around the cars, and I’d still get that feeling that something was off. I still believed my husband was tracking me somehow, and not just him—my boss too. Like they were checking up on where I was or what I was doing, even when I wasn’t in one of those high-energy moods.

So… yes, it keeps going even when my mood feels more normal. It’s not as easy for me to write it off as just being stressed or worked up, because it’s still there during the quieter times.

I’d say… a few weeks, at least. Not months and months straight where everything else felt normal, but there was this quieter stretch—maybe several weeks—where I wasn’t in that revved-up place, I wasn’t making big plans or feeling like I could take on the whole shop by myself.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think… I think it was more like it dropped down, but it didn’t fully go away. Like the volume got turned down, if that makes sense, but the same feeling was still running underneath.

Because even in the quieter stretch, I’d still get that thing where I feel like I’m supposed to be doing more, like I could fix every car in town if people would just stop getting in my way. I wasn’t as loud about it maybe, and I was sleeping more, so from the outside it probably looked like a break. But inside, it didn’t feel totally normal. It was like I was still kind of charged up.

And then little things would set me off. Like if my husband asked why the dishes weren’t done, I’d take it like he was trying to pin me down or make me feel useless, and I’d snap. Or at work, if another tech moved my socket set even a little, I’d get furious, like they were messing with me or trying to slow me down. It felt way bigger than it should have.

So… I guess I’d call it a break from the worst of it, but not really a clean break. The flying-high part was still there in the background, and the irritability definitely was.

I was sleeping more, too, so I thought maybe things were settling down.\n\nBut even then, I still heard him. The garage voice. It would still come through like from the vents or around the cars, and I’d still get that feeling that something was off. I still believed my husband was tracking me somehow, and not just him—my boss too. Like they were checking up on where I was or what I was doing, even when I wasn’t in one of those high-energy moods.\n\nSo… yes, it keeps going even when my mood feels more normal. It’s not as easy for me to write it off as just being stressed or worked up, because it’s still there during the quieter times.\n\nDoctor: Out of the full ten months, can you estimate roughly how many weeks or months you've heard the voice or had those suspicious beliefs while your mood was completely normal—not high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: I’d say… a few weeks, at least. Not months and months straight where everything else felt normal, but there was this quieter stretch—maybe several weeks—where I wasn’t in that revved-up place, I wasn’t making big plans or feeling like I could take on the whole shop by myself.\n\nI was actually sleeping more then, kind of slowed down, so I remember thinking, okay, maybe I’m leveling out. But the voice was still there. I’d still hear him around the garage, like from the vents or near the cars, and I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or checking up on me somehow.\n\nSo if you’re asking when my mood was completely normal… I don’t know if I can say “completely,” because I was scared and tense from it, but I wasn’t high or irritable like before. Around eleven months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the high-energy, irritable stretch has lasted about eleven months, but earlier you described a quieter period where you were sleeping more and not feeling revved up—can you help me understand whether that quieter period was a break from the elevated mood, or if the elevated mood was actually still there in the background even then?\n\nPatient: I think… I think it was more like it dropped down, but it didn’t fully go away. It was like I was still kind of charged up.\n\nAnd then little things would set me off. Like if my husband asked why the dishes weren’t done, I’d take it like he was trying to pin me down or make me feel useless, and I’d snap. Or at work, if another tech moved my socket set even a little, I’d get furious, like they were messing with me or trying to slow me down. It felt way bigger than it should have.\n\nSo… I guess I’d call it a break from the worst of it, but not really a clean break. The flying-high part was still there in the background, and the irritability definitely was.\n\nDoctor: Thinking about that quieter stretch again—was there ever a point, even a few days, where you felt truly like your normal baseline self, with no sense of being 'charged up' or irritable at all, and the voice or the feeling of being tracked was still happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there was a clean point like that, no. I keep trying to separate it out in my head, like, “Was I normal-normal then?” and it’s hard because that quieter stretch did feel different from the really intense part. I was sleeping more, I wasn’t running around making all these huge plans, and I wasn’t acting like I could take over the whole garage or anything.\n\nBut even then… the voice was still there. I’d still hear it around the garage, like it was coming from somewhere it shouldn’t be, and I’d still get that feeling that my husband and my boss were keeping tabs on me or tracking what I was doing. That part didn’t stop just because I was less revved up.\n\nSo if you mean completely baseline, like no charged-up feeling, no irritability at all, and then only the voice and the tracking feeling were happening—I don’t know that I can honestly say yes. It was quieter, but I don’t remember feeling fully like myself. The voice and the suspicious feeling were still happening during those few weeks, though, even when I was sleeping more and not making big plans.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol, drugs (like methamphetamine, cocaine, or marijuana), or new medications/supplements during this same ten-month period?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that in a clean way. I mean, I’m not thinking there was some big new thing I started that explains all of it, if that’s what you mean.\n\nAlcohol… not really anything I’d call heavy. I might have a drink here or there, but I haven’t been on some constant drinking thing. Drugs like meth or cocaine, no. Marijuana, I don’t really think so—not as a regular thing during all this.\n\nNew medications or supplements… I can’t think of anything major. I’m trying to remember because sometimes people act like, “Oh, maybe it was an energy drink or some supplement,” but I don’t have a clear thing like that where I started taking something and then everything changed. So… no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\n[Last Question]\nAside from the high-energy, irritable periods, have you had any stretches during these ten months where you felt persistently sad, hopeless, or down, with low energy or loss of interest in things you normally enjoy?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 32 / automotive_service_technician_or_mechanic / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know that I can point to a real stretch like that, no. I mean… there have been times I felt worn out, or kind of flattened after being so wound up, but I’m not sure I’d call it persistently sad or hopeless in that way.

Like, I’ve had days where I don’t want to deal with people, or I feel tired and fed up, and I’ll think, “I just can’t keep doing this,” but it’s more like frustration or exhaustion than this deep sadness. I still usually have that restless feeling underneath, like I should be doing something or fixing something or pushing through it.

As far as losing interest in things… I don’t know. Sometimes I don’t enjoy stuff the same way, but it’s hard for me to tell if that’s because I’m depressed or because my head is just too busy and I’m irritated all the time. So I wouldn’t say there’s been a clear period where I was just down, low-energy, not interested in anything for weeks. Not that I can really separate out, anyway.

---------- turn 11 | patient:alignment | patient.build_alignment_messages() | model=gpt-5.5 ----------
========== INPUT [patient] ==========

I’m not sure it was before the mood stuff, honestly. That’s the part I keep getting tangled up in, because looking back it all kind of overlaps in my memory. I can say that there were those quieter few weeks where I was sleeping more and I wasn’t doing the huge plans or feeling like I had to take over everything at the garage, and even then the voice was still there.

Like, I would still hear it at the garage, and it still felt like it was coming from somewhere it shouldn’t have been. And I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or watching what I was doing. That didn’t really go away just because I wasn’t as revved up.

But if you’re asking, did I have two full weeks of just the voice and the tracking feeling before I ever noticed any flying-high or irritable feeling… I don’t know that I can say that. I remember the quieter stretch, but I don’t remember a clean beginning where those things came first and the mood came later.

Right, yeah… the best way I can explain it is that even when I wasn’t in that really revved-up state, it didn’t stop.

There were those quieter few weeks where I was sleeping more, and I wasn’t making all those huge plans or acting like I had to run everything at the garage. I wasn’t as “up,” I guess. But the voice was still there. I’d still hear it around the garage, and it still felt like it was coming from somewhere outside of me, not just a thought.

And the tracking feeling didn’t really shut off either. I still believed my husband and my boss were watching me or keeping tabs on what I was doing. It wasn’t like, “Oh, I’m calmer now, so that’s gone.” It stayed there in the background, even during that quieter stretch.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\n(none — this is your first inference)\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, welcome in—thanks for coming today. What brings you in to see me?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s mostly because I keep hearing this man’s voice, and it’s gotten to where I can’t just brush it off anymore. It happens at work a lot, in the garage. It sounds like it’s coming through the vents, like from the ductwork, but then other times I’ll be leaning over a car and it sounds like it’s coming from under the hood, clear as if a guy is standing right next to me talking.\n\nAnd it’s not just noise or like I’m imagining someone called my name. It’s actual words. He tells me not to trust my husband, and sometimes he tells me not to touch certain cars, like there’s something wrong or dangerous about them. I know how that sounds, I really do, but in the moment it feels so real that I freeze up and start looking around the shop trying to figure out where he is. It’s been scaring me, and it’s making it hard to do my job.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing this man's voice—when did it first start?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about ten months, I think. Not years and years, but not just recently either. I remember at first it was easy to tell myself it was the shop, like the vents making sounds or somebody in the next bay talking, so I didn’t really count it as “hearing a voice” right away. But looking back, it started around ten months ago.\n\nAnd the stuff about being tracked or watched—like feeling like there was some reason behind what he was saying—that’s been around that same stretch of time too. It kind of built up, but yeah… about ten months.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you noticed any stretches of time where your mood was significantly different from normal—like feeling very depressed, hopeless, or unusually high-energy and elated—separate from the voices themselves?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. There are definitely times where my mood is not normal for me. It’s hard to separate it from everything because when it’s happening it all feels connected, but I’ll have stretches where I’m just… up. Like I’m flying high, like I can fix every car in town by myself and nobody can touch me at work. I’ll be moving fast, taking on stuff, thinking I can handle everything, and it feels good at first, like I’m finally on top of it.\n\nBut then it flips really quick into being angry. Like if my husband asks something simple, like why the dishes aren’t done, I don’t hear it as simple—I hear it like he’s trying to pin me down or make me smaller. Or at the shop, if another tech moves my socket set even a little, I blow up like they’re trying to mess with me or hold me back. And I know afterward it probably looked like too much, but in the moment it feels completely justified. Like everyone is in my way and I have to push back.\n\nDoctor: During the times when your mood is back to normal—not high or irritable—do you still hear that man's voice or still feel like something's not right with your husband or certain cars?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me, because it doesn’t only happen when I’m all revved up or mad. There were a few weeks where I was quieter, like I wasn’t making all these huge plans or acting like I could take on everything. I was sleeping more, too, so I thought maybe things were settling down.\n\nBut even then, I still heard him. The garage voice. It would still come through like from the vents or around the cars, and I’d still get that feeling that something was off. I still believed my husband was tracking me somehow, and not just him—my boss too. Like they were checking up on where I was or what I was doing, even when I wasn’t in one of those high-energy moods.\n\nSo… yes, it keeps going even when my mood feels more normal. It’s not as easy for me to write it off as just being stressed or worked up, because it’s still there during the quieter times.\n\nDoctor: Out of the full ten months, can you estimate roughly how many weeks or months you've heard the voice or had those suspicious beliefs while your mood was completely normal—not high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: I’d say… a few weeks, at least. Not months and months straight where everything else felt normal, but there was this quieter stretch—maybe several weeks—where I wasn’t in that revved-up place, I wasn’t making big plans or feeling like I could take on the whole shop by myself.\n\nI was actually sleeping more then, kind of slowed down, so I remember thinking, okay, maybe I’m leveling out. But the voice was still there. I’d still hear him around the garage, like from the vents or near the cars, and I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or checking up on me somehow.\n\nSo if you’re asking when my mood was completely normal… I don’t know if I can say “completely,” because I was scared and tense from it, but I wasn’t high or irritable like before. And during that quieter time, it was still happening. Maybe a few weeks.\n\nDoctor: When you were in that 'flying high' and irritable phase, about how many days in a row did that elevated mood and energy last before it changed?\n\nPatient: It wasn’t just a couple days. That’s the thing… I keep trying to make it sound like it came in little bursts, but really the long stretches where I was barely sleeping and my mind was just racing with plans, and I was going nonstop, have been going on for around eleven months.\n\nLike, it might shift in how intense it feels day to day—some days I’m more fired up, some days more irritated—but that “flying high” energy and not needing much sleep has been there for a long time. Around eleven months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the high-energy, irritable stretch has lasted about eleven months, but earlier you described a quieter period where you were sleeping more and not feeling revved up—can you help me understand whether that quieter period was a break from the elevated mood, or if the elevated mood was actually still there in the background even then?\n\nPatient: I think… I think it was more like it dropped down, but it didn’t fully go away. Like the volume got turned down, if that makes sense, but the same feeling was still running underneath.\n\nBecause even in the quieter stretch, I’d still get that thing where I feel like I’m supposed to be doing more, like I could fix every car in town if people would just stop getting in my way. I wasn’t as loud about it maybe, and I was sleeping more, so from the outside it probably looked like a break. But inside, it didn’t feel totally normal. It was like I was still kind of charged up.\n\nAnd then little things would set me off. Like if my husband asked why the dishes weren’t done, I’d take it like he was trying to pin me down or make me feel useless, and I’d snap. Or at work, if another tech moved my socket set even a little, I’d get furious, like they were messing with me or trying to slow me down. It felt way bigger than it should have.\n\nSo… I guess I’d call it a break from the worst of it, but not really a clean break. The flying-high part was still there in the background, and the irritability definitely was.\n\nDoctor: Thinking about that quieter stretch again—was there ever a point, even a few days, where you felt truly like your normal baseline self, with no sense of being 'charged up' or irritable at all, and the voice or the feeling of being tracked was still happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there was a clean point like that, no. I keep trying to separate it out in my head, like, “Was I normal-normal then?” and it’s hard because that quieter stretch did feel different from the really intense part. I was sleeping more, I wasn’t running around making all these huge plans, and I wasn’t acting like I could take over the whole garage or anything.\n\nBut even then… the voice was still there. I’d still hear it around the garage, like it was coming from somewhere it shouldn’t be, and I’d still get that feeling that my husband and my boss were keeping tabs on me or tracking what I was doing. That part didn’t stop just because I was less revved up.\n\nSo if you mean completely baseline, like no charged-up feeling, no irritability at all, and then only the voice and the tracking feeling were happening—I don’t know that I can honestly say yes. It was quieter, but I don’t remember feeling fully like myself. The voice and the suspicious feeling were still happening during those few weeks, though, even when I was sleeping more and not making big plans.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol, drugs (like methamphetamine, cocaine, or marijuana), or new medications/supplements during this same ten-month period?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that in a clean way. I mean, I’m not thinking there was some big new thing I started that explains all of it, if that’s what you mean.\n\nAlcohol… not really anything I’d call heavy. I might have a drink here or there, but I haven’t been on some constant drinking thing. Drugs like meth or cocaine, no. Marijuana, I don’t really think so—not as a regular thing during all this.\n\nNew medications or supplements… I can’t think of anything major. I’m trying to remember because sometimes people act like, “Oh, maybe it was an energy drink or some supplement,” but I don’t have a clear thing like that where I started taking something and then everything changed. So… no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the high-energy, irritable periods, have you had any stretches during these ten months where you felt persistently sad, hopeless, or down, with low energy or loss of interest in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can point to a real stretch like that, no. I mean… there have been times I felt worn out, or kind of flattened after being so wound up, but I’m not sure I’d call it persistently sad or hopeless in that way.\n\nLike, I’ve had days where I don’t want to deal with people, or I feel tired and fed up, and I’ll think, “I just can’t keep doing this,” but it’s more like frustration or exhaustion than this deep sadness. I still usually have that restless feeling underneath, like I should be doing something or fixing something or pushing through it.\n\nAs far as losing interest in things… I don’t know. Sometimes I don’t enjoy stuff the same way, but it’s hard for me to tell if that’s because I’m depressed or because my head is just too busy and I’m irritated all the time. So I wouldn’t say there’s been a clear period where I was just down, low-energy, not interested in anything for weeks. Not that I can really separate out, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Going back to when this first started about ten months ago, was there a period of at least two weeks where you were hearing the voice or feeling tracked before you noticed any of that 'flying high' or irritable mood at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it was before the mood stuff, honestly. That’s the part I keep getting tangled up in, because looking back it all kind of overlaps in my memory. I can say that there were those quieter few weeks where I was sleeping more and I wasn’t doing the huge plans or feeling like I had to take over everything at the garage, and even then the voice was still there.\n\nLike, I would still hear it at the garage, and it still felt like it was coming from somewhere it shouldn’t have been. And I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or watching what I was doing. That didn’t really go away just because I wasn’t as revved up.\n\nBut if you’re asking, did I have two full weeks of just the voice and the tracking feeling before I ever noticed any flying-high or irritable feeling… I don’t know that I can say that. I remember the quieter stretch, but I don’t remember a clean beginning where those things came first and the mood came later.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: Right, yeah… the best way I can explain it is that even when I wasn’t in that really revved-up state, it didn’t stop.\n\nThere were those quieter few weeks where I was sleeping more, and I wasn’t making all those huge plans or acting like I had to run everything at the garage. {"candidates": ["F25.0", "F31.2", "F20.9"], "note": "Patient reports psychotic symptoms (voice, tracking/persecutory delusions) persisting during a quieter period when the classic manic symptoms were reportedly less intense, though not a clean symptom-free baseline. This overlap of near-continuous mood elevation/irritability (~11 months) with psychosis that doesn't fully resolve during less-manic stretches shifts likelihood toward schizoaffective disorder, bipolar type (F25.0), while bipolar I with psychotic features (F31.2) remains possible if the 'quieter' period still represents residual mood pathology rather than true euthymia. Schizophrenia (F20.9) remains a lower-probability candidate given the prominent, prolonged mood component. Substance/medication-induced psychosis effectively ruled out given no reported substance or medication use.", "is_final": false}

I remember at first it was easy to tell myself it was the shop, like the vents making sounds or somebody in the next bay talking, so I didn’t really count it as “hearing a voice” right away. But looking back, it started around ten months ago.\n\nAnd the stuff about being tracked or watched—like feeling like there was some reason behind what he was saying—that’s been around that same stretch of time too. It kind of built up, but yeah… about ten months.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you noticed any stretches of time where your mood was significantly different from normal—like feeling very depressed, hopeless, or unusually high-energy and elated—separate from the voices themselves?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. There are definitely times where my mood is not normal for me. It’s hard to separate it from everything because when it’s happening it all feels connected, but I’ll have stretches where I’m just… up. Like I’m flying high, like I can fix every car in town by myself and nobody can touch me at work. I’ll be moving fast, taking on stuff, thinking I can handle everything, and it feels good at first, like I’m finally on top of it.\n\nBut then it flips really quick into being angry. Like if my husband asks something simple, like why the dishes aren’t done, I don’t hear it as simple—I hear it like he’s trying to pin me down or make me smaller. Or at the shop, if another tech moves my socket set even a little, I blow up like they’re trying to mess with me or hold me back. And I know afterward it probably looked like too much, but in the moment it feels completely justified. Like everyone is in my way and I have to push back.\n\nDoctor: During the times when your mood is back to normal—not high or irritable—do you still hear that man's voice or still feel like something's not right with your husband or certain cars?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me, because it doesn’t only happen when I’m all revved up or mad. There were a few weeks where I was quieter, like I wasn’t making all these huge plans or acting like I could take on everything. I was sleeping more, too, so I thought maybe things were settling down.\n\nBut even then, I still heard him. The garage voice. It would still come through like from the vents or around the cars, and I’d still get that feeling that something was off. I still believed my husband was tracking me somehow, and not just him—my boss too. Like they were checking up on where I was or what I was doing, even when I wasn’t in one of those high-energy moods.\n\nSo… yes, it keeps going even when my mood feels more normal. It’s not as easy for me to write it off as just being stressed or worked up, because it’s still there during the quieter times.\n\nDoctor: Out of the full ten months, can you estimate roughly how many weeks or months you've heard the voice or had those suspicious beliefs while your mood was completely normal—not high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: I’d say… a few weeks, at least. Not months and months straight where everything else felt normal, but there was this quieter stretch—maybe several weeks—where I wasn’t in that revved-up place, I wasn’t making big plans or feeling like I could take on the whole shop by myself.\n\nI was actually sleeping more then, kind of slowed down, so I remember thinking, okay, maybe I’m leveling out. But the voice was still there. I’d still hear him around the garage, like from the vents or near the cars, and I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or checking up on me somehow.\n\nSo if you’re asking when my mood was completely normal… I don’t know if I can say “completely,” because I was scared and tense from it, but I wasn’t high or irritable like before. And during that quieter time, it was still happening. Maybe a few weeks.\n\nDoctor: When you were in that 'flying high' and irritable phase, about how many days in a row did that elevated mood and energy last before it changed?\n\nPatient: It wasn’t just a couple days. That’s the thing… I keep trying to make it sound like it came in little bursts, but really the long stretches where I was barely sleeping and my mind was just racing with plans, and I was going nonstop, have been going on for around eleven months.\n\nLike, it might shift in how intense it feels day to day—some days I’m more fired up, some days more irritated—but that “flying high” energy and not needing much sleep has been there for a long time. Around eleven months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the high-energy, irritable stretch has lasted about eleven months, but earlier you described a quieter period where you were sleeping more and not feeling revved up—can you help me understand whether that quieter period was a break from the elevated mood, or if the elevated mood was actually still there in the background even then?\n\nPatient: I think… I think it was more like it dropped down, but it didn’t fully go away. Like the volume got turned down, if that makes sense, but the same feeling was still running underneath.\n\nBecause even in the quieter stretch, I’d still get that thing where I feel like I’m supposed to be doing more, like I could fix every car in town if people would just stop getting in my way. I wasn’t as loud about it maybe, and I was sleeping more, so from the outside it probably looked like a break. But inside, it didn’t feel totally normal. It was like I was still kind of charged up.\n\nAnd then little things would set me off. Like if my husband asked why the dishes weren’t done, I’d take it like he was trying to pin me down or make me feel useless, and I’d snap. Or at work, if another tech moved my socket set even a little, I’d get furious, like they were messing with me or trying to slow me down. It felt way bigger than it should have.\n\nSo… I guess I’d call it a break from the worst of it, but not really a clean break. The flying-high part was still there in the background, and the irritability definitely was.\n\nDoctor: Thinking about that quieter stretch again—was there ever a point, even a few days, where you felt truly like your normal baseline self, with no sense of being 'charged up' or irritable at all, and the voice or the feeling of being tracked was still happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there was a clean point like that, no. I keep trying to separate it out in my head, like, “Was I normal-normal then?” and it’s hard because that quieter stretch did feel different from the really intense part. I was sleeping more, I wasn’t running around making all these huge plans, and I wasn’t acting like I could take over the whole garage or anything.\n\nBut even then… the voice was still there. I’d still hear it around the garage, like it was coming from somewhere it shouldn’t be, and I’d still get that feeling that my husband and my boss were keeping tabs on me or tracking what I was doing. It was quieter, but I don’t remember feeling fully like myself. The voice and the suspicious feeling were still happening during those few weeks, though, even when I was sleeping more and not making big plans.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol, drugs (like methamphetamine, cocaine, or marijuana), or new medications/supplements during this same ten-month period?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that in a clean way. I might have a drink here or there, but I haven’t been on some constant drinking thing. Drugs like meth or cocaine, no. Marijuana, I don’t really think so—not as a regular thing during all this.\n\nNew medications or supplements… I can’t think of anything major. I’m trying to remember because sometimes people act like, “Oh, maybe it was an energy drink or some supplement,” but I don’t have a clear thing like that where I started taking something and then everything changed. So… no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the high-energy, irritable periods, have you had any stretches during these ten months where you felt persistently sad, hopeless, or down, with low energy or loss of interest in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can point to a real stretch like that, no. I mean… there have been times I felt worn out, or kind of flattened after being so wound up, but I’m not sure I’d call it persistently sad or hopeless in that way.\n\nLike, I’ve had days where I don’t want to deal with people, or I feel tired and fed up, and I’ll think, “I just can’t keep doing this,” but it’s more like frustration or exhaustion than this deep sadness. I still usually have that restless feeling underneath, like I should be doing something or fixing something or pushing through it.\n\nAs far as losing interest in things… I don’t know. Sometimes I don’t enjoy stuff the same way, but it’s hard for me to tell if that’s because I’m depressed or because my head is just too busy and I’m irritated all the time. What brings you in to see me?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s mostly because I keep hearing this man’s voice, and it’s gotten to where I can’t just brush it off anymore. It happens at work a lot, in the garage. It sounds like it’s coming through the vents, like from the ductwork, but then other times I’ll be leaning over a car and it sounds like it’s coming from under the hood, clear as if a guy is standing right next to me talking.\n\nAnd it’s not just noise or like I’m imagining someone called my name. It’s actual words. He tells me not to trust my husband, and sometimes he tells me not to touch certain cars, like there’s something wrong or dangerous about them. I know how that sounds, I really do, but in the moment it feels so real that I freeze up and start looking around the shop trying to figure out where he is. It’s been scaring me, and it’s making it hard to do my job.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been hearing this man's voice—when did it first start?\n\nPatient: It’s been… about ten months, I think. Not years and years, but not just recently either. I remember at first it was easy to tell myself it was the shop, like the vents making sounds or somebody in the next bay talking, so I didn’t really count it as “hearing a voice” right away. But looking back, it started around ten months ago.\n\nAnd the stuff about being tracked or watched—like feeling like there was some reason behind what he was saying—that’s been around that same stretch of time too. It kind of built up, but yeah… about ten months.\n\nDoctor: During these past ten months, have you noticed any stretches of time where your mood was significantly different from normal—like feeling very depressed, hopeless, or unusually high-energy and elated—separate from the voices themselves?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. There are definitely times where my mood is not normal for me. It’s hard to separate it from everything because when it’s happening it all feels connected, but I’ll have stretches where I’m just… up. Like I’m flying high, like I can fix every car in town by myself and nobody can touch me at work. I’ll be moving fast, taking on stuff, thinking I can handle everything, and it feels good at first, like I’m finally on top of it.\n\nBut then it flips really quick into being angry. Like if my husband asks something simple, like why the dishes aren’t done, I don’t hear it as simple—I hear it like he’s trying to pin me down or make me smaller. Or at the shop, if another tech moves my socket set even a little, I blow up like they’re trying to mess with me or hold me back. And I know afterward it probably looked like too much, but in the moment it feels completely justified. Like everyone is in my way and I have to push back.\n\nDoctor: During the times when your mood is back to normal—not high or irritable—do you still hear that man's voice or still feel like something's not right with your husband or certain cars?\n\nPatient: Yeah… that’s the part that scares me, because it doesn’t only happen when I’m all revved up or mad. There were a few weeks where I was quieter, like I wasn’t making all these huge plans or acting like I could take on everything. I was sleeping more, too, so I thought maybe things were settling down.\n\nBut even then, I still heard him. The garage voice. It would still come through like from the vents or around the cars, and I’d still get that feeling that something was off. I still believed my husband was tracking me somehow, and not just him—my boss too. Like they were checking up on where I was or what I was doing, even when I wasn’t in one of those high-energy moods.\n\nSo… yes, it keeps going even when my mood feels more normal. It’s not as easy for me to write it off as just being stressed or worked up, because it’s still there during the quieter times.\n\nDoctor: Out of the full ten months, can you estimate roughly how many weeks or months you've heard the voice or had those suspicious beliefs while your mood was completely normal—not high, irritable, or depressed?\n\nPatient: I’d say… a few weeks, at least. Not months and months straight where everything else felt normal, but there was this quieter stretch—maybe several weeks—where I wasn’t in that revved-up place, I wasn’t making big plans or feeling like I could take on the whole shop by myself.\n\nI was actually sleeping more then, kind of slowed down, so I remember thinking, okay, maybe I’m leveling out. But the voice was still there. I’d still hear him around the garage, like from the vents or near the cars, and I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or checking up on me somehow.\n\nSo if you’re asking when my mood was completely normal… I don’t know if I can say “completely,” because I was scared and tense from it, but I wasn’t high or irritable like before. And during that quieter time, it was still happening. Maybe a few weeks.\n\nDoctor: When you were in that 'flying high' and irritable phase, about how many days in a row did that elevated mood and energy last before it changed?\n\nPatient: It wasn’t just a couple days. That’s the thing… I keep trying to make it sound like it came in little bursts, but really the long stretches where I was barely sleeping and my mind was just racing with plans, and I was going nonstop, have been going on for around eleven months.\n\nLike, it might shift in how intense it feels day to day—some days I’m more fired up, some days more irritated—but that “flying high” energy and not needing much sleep has been there for a long time. Around eleven months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned the high-energy, irritable stretch has lasted about eleven months, but earlier you described a quieter period where you were sleeping more and not feeling revved up—can you help me understand whether that quieter period was a break from the elevated mood, or if the elevated mood was actually still there in the background even then?\n\nPatient: I think… I think it was more like it dropped down, but it didn’t fully go away. Like the volume got turned down, if that makes sense, but the same feeling was still running underneath.\n\nBecause even in the quieter stretch, I’d still get that thing where I feel like I’m supposed to be doing more, like I could fix every car in town if people would just stop getting in my way. I wasn’t as loud about it maybe, and I was sleeping more, so from the outside it probably looked like a break. But inside, it didn’t feel totally normal. It was like I was still kind of charged up.\n\nAnd then little things would set me off. Like if my husband asked why the dishes weren’t done, I’d take it like he was trying to pin me down or make me feel useless, and I’d snap. Or at work, if another tech moved my socket set even a little, I’d get furious, like they were messing with me or trying to slow me down. It felt way bigger than it should have.\n\nSo… I guess I’d call it a break from the worst of it, but not really a clean break. The flying-high part was still there in the background, and the irritability definitely was.\n\nDoctor: Thinking about that quieter stretch again—was there ever a point, even a few days, where you felt truly like your normal baseline self, with no sense of being 'charged up' or irritable at all, and the voice or the feeling of being tracked was still happening?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there was a clean point like that, no. I keep trying to separate it out in my head, like, “Was I normal-normal then?” and it’s hard because that quieter stretch did feel different from the really intense part. I was sleeping more, I wasn’t running around making all these huge plans, and I wasn’t acting like I could take over the whole garage or anything.\n\nBut even then… the voice was still there. I’d still hear it around the garage, like it was coming from somewhere it shouldn’t be, and I’d still get that feeling that my husband and my boss were keeping tabs on me or tracking what I was doing. That part didn’t stop just because I was less revved up.\n\nSo if you mean completely baseline, like no charged-up feeling, no irritability at all, and then only the voice and the tracking feeling were happening—I don’t know that I can honestly say yes. It was quieter, but I don’t remember feeling fully like myself. The voice and the suspicious feeling were still happening during those few weeks, though, even when I was sleeping more and not making big plans.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using any alcohol, drugs (like methamphetamine, cocaine, or marijuana), or new medications/supplements during this same ten-month period?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that in a clean way. I mean, I’m not thinking there was some big new thing I started that explains all of it, if that’s what you mean.\n\nAlcohol… not really anything I’d call heavy. I might have a drink here or there, but I haven’t been on some constant drinking thing. Drugs like meth or cocaine, no. Marijuana, I don’t really think so—not as a regular thing during all this.\n\nNew medications or supplements… I can’t think of anything major. I’m trying to remember because sometimes people act like, “Oh, maybe it was an energy drink or some supplement,” but I don’t have a clear thing like that where I started taking something and then everything changed. So… no, not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Aside from the high-energy, irritable periods, have you had any stretches during these ten months where you felt persistently sad, hopeless, or down, with low energy or loss of interest in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can point to a real stretch like that, no. I mean… there have been times I felt worn out, or kind of flattened after being so wound up, but I’m not sure I’d call it persistently sad or hopeless in that way.\n\nLike, I’ve had days where I don’t want to deal with people, or I feel tired and fed up, and I’ll think, “I just can’t keep doing this,” but it’s more like frustration or exhaustion than this deep sadness. I still usually have that restless feeling underneath, like I should be doing something or fixing something or pushing through it.\n\nAs far as losing interest in things… I don’t know. Sometimes I don’t enjoy stuff the same way, but it’s hard for me to tell if that’s because I’m depressed or because my head is just too busy and I’m irritated all the time. So I wouldn’t say there’s been a clear period where I was just down, low-energy, not interested in anything for weeks. Not that I can really separate out, anyway.\n\nDoctor: Going back to when this first started about ten months ago, was there a period of at least two weeks where you were hearing the voice or feeling tracked before you noticed any of that 'flying high' or irritable mood at all?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it was before the mood stuff, honestly. That’s the part I keep getting tangled up in, because looking back it all kind of overlaps in my memory. I can say that there were those quieter few weeks where I was sleeping more and I wasn’t doing the huge plans or feeling like I had to take over everything at the garage, and even then the voice was still there.\n\nLike, I would still hear it at the garage, and it still felt like it was coming from somewhere it shouldn’t have been. And I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or watching what I was doing. That didn’t really go away just because I wasn’t as revved up.\n\nBut if you’re asking, did I have two full weeks of just the voice and the tracking feeling before I ever noticed any flying-high or irritable feeling… I don’t know that I can say that. I remember the quieter stretch, but I don’t remember a clean beginning where those things came first and the mood came later.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: Right, yeah… the best way I can explain it is that even when I wasn’t in that really revved-up state, it didn’t stop.\n\nThere were those quieter few weeks where I was sleeping more, and I wasn’t making all those huge plans or acting like I had to run everything at the garage. I wasn’t as “up,” I guess. But the voice was still there. I’d still hear it around the garage, and it still felt like it was coming from somewhere outside of me, not just a thought.\n\nAnd the tracking feeling didn’t really shut off either. I still believed my husband and my boss were watching me or keeping tabs on what I was doing. It wasn’t like, “Oh, I’m calmer now, so that’s gone.” It stayed there in the background, even during that quieter stretch.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: Yeah… I know I’m kind of repeating myself, but that quieter period is the clearest example I can give.\n\nIt wasn’t like I was completely fine, but I wasn’t in that intense mode where I was barely sleeping or making these big plans or feeling like I had to be on top of everything at the garage. I was sleeping more for a few weeks, and I wasn’t pushing so hard or acting like everything depended on me.\n\nBut even then, the voice was still happening. I’d still hear it at the garage, and it still felt like it was coming from outside me, not like my own thoughts. And I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or keeping tabs on me. That part didn’t really go away just because I was quieter or less wound up. It was still there, kind of running underneath everything.\n\nDoctor: During that quieter, more-sleep stretch, did you ever go a few days with absolutely no irritability or snapping at people—truly calm and even-tempered—while still hearing the voice or feeling tracked, or was there always at least some irritability or restlessness present even then?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can honestly separate it that cleanly, like “these three days I was perfectly calm.” I wish I could, but it’s blurry.\n\nWhat I do know is during that quieter stretch, when I was sleeping more and not doing all the big plans or trying to take charge of everything at the garage, the voice was still there. I would still hear it at work, and it still felt like it wasn’t just me thinking something. And I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or watching what I was doing.\n\nSo even if I seemed calmer on the outside, that part didn’t stop. I can’t swear there were days with absolutely no irritability or restlessness, because I was still tense from feeling watched, if that makes sense. But the voice and the tracking feeling were definitely still happening during the quieter few weeks.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [doctor] ==========
{"candidates": ["F31.2"], "note": "Patient cannot identify a clear period of at least two weeks where psychotic symptoms occurred in the absence of mood symptoms (irritability/restlessness persisted even during the 'quieter' phase). This makes a mood disorder with psychotic features (bipolar I, manic with psychotic features) more likely than schizoaffective disorder, which requires a distinct period of psychosis without prominent mood symptoms. Ruling out F25.0.", "is_final": true}

And I still had that feeling that my husband and my boss were tracking me or watching what I was doing.\n\nSo even if I seemed calmer on the outside, that part didn’t stop.
